## GRAND PRIX PROVIDENCE 2013

![](https://i.ormos.uk/93/7a/937aaaca5098d3247772fa043160a4c6b36151b2c65579134f2e085a1bd02907.jpg)

After an incredibly long day, consisting of five different drafts, the Finals went by in a relative blur. Andrew Longo, Eric Phillipps, and Eric Berger defeated Matt McCullough, Ari Lax, and Alex John in a quick two-set match fraught with punishing draws, aggressive openings, and stifling defense.

This trio of friends from the New York area used many of the same tricks they had used to make it to Day 2 to help them become the Grand Prix Providence champions. One of Phillipps's all-stars from his Finals deck was [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa) of Vizkopa, the same card he used to run roughshod over the Finals. Longo's Finals deck made great use of [Unflinching Courage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unflinching+Courage), which Berger had used to destroy two different drafts earlier in the day. But the biggest card in the Finals was certainly Phillipps's [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience), which managed to slow down the stupidly aggressive decks Lax and McCullough drafted just enough for him to take over.

This weekend was a veritable who's who of the past and present of East Coast **Magic**, and it was fitting that an East Coast team would be the ones to take home the trophy. They drafted well, they played well, and they also displayed their fair share of, well, providence on their way to becoming the newest team to take home a Grand Prix title here at Grand Prix Providence.

### Eric Berger

**Age:** 34  
**Hometown:** New York, NY  
**Occupation:** Patent Lawyer  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 Started PT San Diego 2007 4-0. Convinced an amazing woman to marry me despite playing too much **Magic**.

**Guild Affiliation:**  
 Boros

**Who are your teammates and why did you play with them?**  
 Eric Phillipps and Andy Longo, who we picked up instead of Josh Ravitz, who chose to play with Chris Pikula and Igor Frayman.

**What color combination did you play in Sealed, and what was your best card?**  
 RGB. [Armed & Dangerous](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armed+%2F%2F+Dangerous).

**What color combinations did you draft, and what were your best cards in each?**  
 1st – GUW – [Unflinching Courage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unflinching+Courage)  
 2nd – GUW – [Unflinching Courage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unflinching+Courage)  
 3rd – WB – [Boros Reckoner](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boros+Reckoner), [Pontiff of Blight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pontiff+of+Blight), 2x [Grisly Spectacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grisly+Spectacle)

**If you could team up with a Planeswalker for the next Team Limited Grand Prix, which Planeswalker would you choose and why?**  
 I have no idea.

**Age:** 35

 Top 8 US Nationals, New England Regional Champion 1998, money finishes in a few PTs.

 Dimir

 Kevin Bohlmann and Bruce Cowley. They're friends who play **Magic** well and we get along.

**What color combinations did you draft, and what were your best cards in each?**

 Jace. He's the best.

**Age:** 35  
**Hometown:** NYC

 UGr, [Progenitor Mimic](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Progenitor+Mimic)

**What color combinations did you draft, and what were your best cards in each?**  
 1st – BWU – [Ogre Slumlord](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ogre+Slumlord)  
 2nd – BWU – One-Thousand Lashes  
 3rd – UGb – [Dinrova Horror](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dinrova+Horror)

**If you could team up with a Planeswalker for the next Team Limited Grand Prix, which Planeswalker would you choose and why?**  
[Nicol Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nicol+Bolas), not close.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6c/1a/6c1aedb0dd2c12cebad5e47353c871e532bf376a90443cf0ce7eace2e36636e7.jpg)

### Ari Lax

**Age:** 22  
**Hometown:** Boston  
**Occupation:** Systems Engineer  
  

**Previous **Magic** Accomplishments:**  
 2 Pro Tour Top 16s4 Grand Prix Top 8s

**Guild Affiliation:**  
 Dimir

**Who are your teammates and why did you play with them?**  
 Alex (John) is responsible for both my Limited GP Top 8s, and Matt (McCullough) is about 70% in PT drafts lifetime. Secret team captain was DJ Kastner.

**What color combination did you play in Sealed, and what was your best card?**  
 Gruul with [Domri Rade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Domri+Rade)

**What color combinations did you draft, and what were your best cards in each?**

 2nd – WR – [Warleader's Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Warleader%27s+Helix) (2-0)  
 3rd – GRB – [Explosive Impact](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explosive+Impact) (2-0)

**If you could team up for a Planeswalker for the next Team Limited Grand Prix, which Planeswalker would you choose and why?**  
 Bo Levar. He seems like the nut.

## Team Draft Chart: All the Players, All the Picks!

*by Nate Price & Tobi Henke*

 

Take a look at the following table. The vertical columns have all picks by the player listed at the very top, one after the other. The same shade of grey here denotes cards taken from the same booster pack. That's to help you see what choices the player faced when he made a particular pick. By going along the diagonal line of same-colored boxes downwards, you can see what cards were left in the booster at any point in the draft.

## Top 4 - Semifinals: Bruce Cowley, Christopher Manning, and Kevin Bohlmann vs. Matt McCullough, Ari Lax, and Alexander John

*by Mike Rosenberg*

 

Three players sat down, but only two matches reached their natural conclusion. Matt McCullough and Ari Lax were able to defeat Bruce Cowley and Christopher Manning in their matches, leaving the second game of Alexander John's match against Kevin Bohlmann unnecessary and unresolved.

### Cowley and McCullough's Match

The first match to finish was between Cowley and McCullough, where McCullough slowly whittled Cowley's Gruul forces down through huge swings in resources thanks to [Midnight Recovery](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Midnight+Recovery) and [Sepulchral Primordial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sepulchral+Primordial). The three creatures [Midnight Recovery](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Midnight+Recovery) brought back and the Primordial left Cowley out of gas and racing an unwinnable clock.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1a/ee/1aeeee422f46fd98d65fbb0108fa381744ed5144a053097a9542a2cb3d6ce083.jpg)  
*Bruce Cowley, Christopher Manning, and Kevin Bohlmann* 

The second game left Cowley without red mana and stranded on three lands, as an enraged [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout) and [Millennial Gargoyle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Millennial+Gargoyle) beat down until Cowley had enough. The two players quickly joined their teammates to aid in any difficult decisions.

### Bohlmann and John's Match

While their teammates were blowing through each game at a rapid pace, Bohlmann and John ended up in quick board stalls. [Paralyzing Grasp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Paralyzing+Grasp) and [Runner's Bane](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runner%27s+Bane) locked down [Kraul Warrior](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kraul+Warrior) and [Gyre Sage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gyre+Sage) respectively, and all of John's other blue creatures looked very awkward in the face of [Skylasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skylasher) from Bohlmann.

However, one of those blue creatures was also red, and had plenty of abilities for John to use. [Nivix Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nivix+Guildmage) helped John sculpt a hand that could actually...well, do something. As Bohlmann built up an offensive force with Hunter of Nightveil and [Maze Glider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maze+Glider), which let the Hunter swing into the air, John started evolving his [Gyre Sage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gyre+Sage). Eventually, it hit 4/5, freeing it from the [Runner's Bane](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runner%27s+Bane). Once it was untapped, a timely [Explosive Impact](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explosive+Impact) shot down the [Maze Glider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maze+Glider) mid-combat, which dropped the formerly flying Hunter of Nightveil back down to earth where [Gyre Sage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gyre+Sage) could promptly smash it.

After that, it was elementary. John continued to sculpt his hand, while Bohlmann attempted to mount some sort of pressure. Eventually, John found what he was looking for, as a bloodrushed [Skarrg Goliath](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skarrg+Goliath) when John sent in his team pushed through the final points of damage once Bohlmann was tapped out.

The second game looked to go just as long, if it were not for...

### Manning and Lax's Match

It seemed like the team's match would be settled on the result of the games between Bohlmann and John after Lax had a not-so-stellar start against Manning in their match. Despite having a [Martial Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martial+Law) active, a [Purge the Profane](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Purge+the+Profane) deprived Lax of necessary tricks, and creatures like [Precinct Captain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Precinct+Captain) were getting dwarfed by centaur tokens via Manning's Courser's Accord. The one major threat Lax was able to present, a [Tenement Crasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tenement+Crasher), was given [One Thousand Lashes](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=One+Thousand+Lashes).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b4/d3/b4d31a6f7502225ab58e95ac75f0412e8a4023bb640c8f87aea48386076698e7.jpg)  
*Matt McCullough, Ari Lax, and Alexander John* 

While it took a few turns for the game to end, the winner was clear as Manning's board grew and Lax's board remained stagnant. [Martial Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martial+Law) didn't buy Lax time; it simply slowed his demise.

That could not be said for the second game, where [Martial Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martial+Law) helped allow [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) and [Warmind Infantry](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Warmind+Infantry) to push through for early damage. Courser's Accord gave him even more beef to attack with, and while Manning was able to take out the centaur tokens and [Warmind Infantry](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Warmind+Infantry) over the course of a couple of turns, he could not stop dying to the [Massive Raid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Massive+Raid) that came after a couple more creatures entered play.

The third and final game saw Maninng staring down a brutal curve consisting of [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace), [Skyknight Legionnaire](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skyknight+Legionnaire), and [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade). While [Avenging Arrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenging+Arrow) shot down the [Fencing Ace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fencing+Ace), [Martial Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martial+Law) from Lax eventually causes some havoc, detaining Manning's best creature each turn.

Eventually, it detained a centaur token while Manning was at 8 with a [Daggerdrome Imp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daggerdrome+Imp). When Lax attacked with a [Steeple Roc](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steeple+Roc), Manning declined to block, and lax was quick to slam a [Scorchwalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scorchwalker) onto the table for its bloodrush. Without any answers to the now 8 damage coming his way, Manning offered the concession.

* * *

## Finals - Round 1 – McCullough/Lax/John vs. Longo/Phillipps/Berger

*by Nate Price*

 

When is having five [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade)s not necessarily a good thing? When they come into play tapped, if even at all.

While Alex John's intense match against Eric Berger was just heating up, his teammates were catching some real coolers. John and Berger only managed to finish one very interesting, grindy game before both Ari Lax and Matt McCullough had dropped their matches to Andrew Longo and Eric Phillipps without even winning a game.

"There was nothing I could do," McCullough said in shock as he turned to watch the final turns of Lax's game against Phillipps. McCullough had mulliganned in the first game into a hand that failed to see a third land, resulting in the first game loss of the match. The second game saw Longo play out an incredibly aggressive draw from his Bant deck, pushing McCullough's creatures out of the way with [Runner's Bane](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runner%27s+Bane) and securing his team the first win of the first round.

Phillipps, meanwhile, took his first game on the back of [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience), perhaps the perfect card against Lax's turbo-charged Boros deck.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/50/35/5035f5a55cdb4dd1f927dfa9fccc6074626a51ff81c0987dc7c0ade4dd0e628d.jpg)  
*Ari Lax, Matt McCullough, Alex John* 

"That card's pretty good against this guy," Lax said as he put an unimpressive [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) into play tapped. The [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience) did work, keeping Lax slowed down enough that Phillipps was able to get himself an offense going. [Assault Griffin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Assault+Griffin) Took to the skies, while a stream of chump blockers both impeded Lax and gave Phillipps the triggers he needed to keep afloat. Eventually, Phillipps was able to finish things off with a Toil and Extort. Lax's lone Plains contributed to his defeat, but the all-star of that game was certainly the Obedience.

The second game was a bit better for Lax initially, as his creatures didn't have to slow down through the toll lane. Phillipps managed to keep afloat by nearly matching Lax creature-for-creature, but cards like [Rubblebelt Maaka](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rubblebelt+Maaka) ensured that Lax's creatures came out ahead. In a bit of trouble, Phillipps managed to get himself back on his feet thanks to another powerful Orzhov rare.

"Well, they have a [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa)," Lax said as the powerful mythic hit the table.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e1/d3/e1d3fed693e8642f3881427bde05dff60feae9222aed22f5521614ee4f0d90bf.jpg)  
*Eric Berger, Andrew Longo, Eric Phillipps* 

"As in [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa)[Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa)," John asked.

Lax confirmed, and the lifelinker began to do work, keeping his life total more or less static while taking chunks out of Lax's. It swing in for a few turns before running into a [Skinbrand Goblin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skinbrand+Goblin) and [Riot Piker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Piker) that Lax had just cast, his only non-white creatures.

"Yep, that'll do it," Phillipps said, putting the Baron into his graveyard.

"Really?! Did this just happen?!" Lax was stunned. "We've got a game, boys!"

![Blood Baron of Vizkopa](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa)

He was even more stunned when the [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa) came back via [Obzedat's Aid](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obzedat%27s+Aid) a turn later. From that point, the match was effectively over. A couple of swings and the match had ended, ending the round before John and Berger could even draw their hands for the second game. That was unfortunate, as both decks were very powerful and impressive, and their first game was so interesting.

While Berger started with a quick assault of two-powered creatures, John was able to significantly slow the clock with [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil). Berger's offense consisted of creatures that were still able to profitably attack, but the bleeding had slowed to half. Eventually, a [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) provided another effective blocker, slowing things down even more. Berger then found himself a backdoor to get at John's life total with a [Rogue's Passage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rogue%27s+Passage). Sitting at a low 4 life, John found a [Dinrova Horror](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dinrova+Horror) to effectively destroy a Rogues Passage that was slowly making work of his final life points. A Maze Flier a couple of turns later made the largest of his creatures fliers, and he suddenly found himself with an offense. Two attacks later, Berger was reaching for his sideboard.

It was an incredible switch in fortunes, and it required some very careful management of both life total and creatures on the board by John. Unfortunately, there would be no repeat performance. Right as John was asking for an assessment of his sideboarding plan, the [Blood Baron of Vizkopa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Baron+of+Vizkopa) hit home for the final time.

"Oh. So we're done," he said, slumping in his chair.

* * *

## Finals Round 2 Andrew Longo, Eric Philipps, and Eric Berger vs. Matt McCullough, Ari Lax, and Alexander

*by Mike Rosenberg*

 

Despite Ari Lax's protests in his second game, where he was fighting on near equal footing against a second-turn [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) for a long time, his teammates Matt McCullough and Alexander John were just unable to hold on. Andrew Longo, Eric Philipps, and Eric Berger took down their second and final match against the Michigan team to become the champions of Grand Prix Providence.

Lax's match against Berger went undecided, as the two matches from his teammates did not let him finish the second game, despite his protests in hoping to beat Berger's rare-packed (and [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) driven) Golgari deck.

### Longo and John's Match

John looked to have the upper hand in his match against Longo, who had a very aggressive Bant deck with early beaters like [Call of the Conclave](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Conclave) and solid cards all around. [Simic Fluxmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Simic+Fluxmage), a card that McCullough and his team had been talking about with much praise, did some work in this game, growing a [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil) and keeping the pressure on.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/72/42/724251629386edd35e2c2a3461a69cac820ec0966bcaf5e8d7ca579f4eaeb8e8.jpg)  
*Eric Berger, Eric Philipps, and Andrew Longo* 

Then, lands started coming. A lot of them. It seemed like Longo got a reprieve in the first game, until the fused [Beck](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Beck) & Call gave John a lot of uses for his mana. Longo, having suffered some bleeding in the first few turns of the game, conceded to the overwhelmed draw effect and birds that he couldn't fend off.

The second game, however, went the other way, as Longo's deck came out of the gates swinging. Within minutes, Long had moved to a third game...

...a game in which his deck didn't deliver a third land to turn on his hand, even despite digging with [Prophetic Prism](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Prophetic+Prism). Normally, an opponent playing a third creature could be bad news for a mana starved player, but it wasn't the immediate end of the game.

In this case, however, it was. The [Call of the Conclave](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Conclave) token that Longo had made on the second turn gained [Unflinching Courage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unflinching+Courage), and suddenly the game was over. John had no answers to the 5/5 token in hand, and his [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars) went from looking reasonable to looking really embarrassing.

With a few short turns, John showed his hand of spells and offered the handshake.

### Philipps and McCullough's Match

McCullough's Boros deck had some serious aggression. The key feature of his list was three [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade)s, which was 60% of the Firstblades opened in the draft. Philipps however had [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience), a card that stopped Firstblade's impact cold.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/8e/b3/8eb3e867c7f3bbf9dbdc6c05456ceaaa960477b0f0bb676e72ed54f4b9d038a6.jpg)  
*Ari Lax, Matt McCullough, and Alexander John* 

This ended up not even being a factor, with the [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience) coming down a turn after MucCullough played and attacked with his first (and only) [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) in the second game. It was a stronger start than what McCullough saw in the first game, but much like the first game, McCullough's hand did not improve with more action. His Boros deck began to see land after land, while Philipps filled the board up with higher quality creatures, getting some extortion in thanks to his enchantment each time he played a play.

When the writing was on the wall, McCullough fell deep into thought. At that point, Lax was fighting for honor, hoping to defeat Berger's Pack rat. "Don't scoop yet, Matt!" he shouted, as his teammate looked up. "Thanks for the encouragement," he replied.

Lax didn't care, knowing that his team was at its end. "But I want to win this one!"

Once Lax's fight against the [Pack Rat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pack+Rat) came to an unfortunate end, McCullough went through his turn and offered the concession when nothing further was coming.

And after two quick rounds of finals action, and after nearly 16 hours of Day Two play, that was it. Longo, Philipps, and Berger locked up the win and were crowned as the champions of Grand Prix Providence!

* * *

## Top 5 Cards

 

[Gruul War Chant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gruul+War+Chant) might make this list. I recall Round 8 feature match where seeing it caused Nakamura to go from a board state that was winnable to a board state where he's down to zero outs.

 

 

![Scab-Clan Giant](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Scab-Clan+Giant)

 

### 5\. [Scab-Clan Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scab-Clan+Giant)

"Time to spin the wheel!" Luis Scott-Vargas

This card was absolutely all over the place in our video and text coverage throughout the weekend. Like some sort of magical hawkeye, it never seemed to miss the target, blowing up creature after creature, and leaving a massive 4/5 body in its wake. While the random element is certainly a drawback, as you don't have control over what you hit and you might hit a creature big enough to kill it, the random nature led to some of the more fun player interactions in the tournament. Luis Scott-Vargas managed to coax a smile out of the normally stoic Michael Jacob with some die rolling antics, as they decided which creature the Giant would hit. It wouldn't be the last time a player cracked a smile over the "big gamble" himself.

For contributing to the fun of this already fun weekend, being ubiquitous on our cameras, and being a boss of a big basher, the Giant is the first entry into Grand Prix Providence's Top 5 cards of the weekend.

  
  

 

![Haunter of Nightveil](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Haunter+of+Nightveil)

 

### 4\. [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil)

There are few cards that are able to take control of a game like the [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil) is. Completely shutting down most of the fastest decks in the format, the Haunter is a nightmare for any deck that relies on swarms of creatures to win the game, which is effectively every deck except Simic and Gruul. Losing one point of power per creature can often add up to as much as four or five life virtually gained a turn.

A great example of this is from the one game Eric Berger and Alex John were able to play in their first Finals match. Facing down an army of two-power creatures, John was able to completely stabilize with a single [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil). He halved Berger's offensive power and gained a big enough body to hold the remaining attackers off. In the end, it was what he needed to assemble a couple more massive bodies to take his game.

Enabling decks to come back from seemingly dire circumstances, such as this, is why the [Haunter of Nightveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Haunter+of+Nightveil) earned its spot on this list.

  
  

 

![Rubblebelt Maaka](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Rubblebelt+Maaka)

 

### 3\. [Rubblebelt Maaka](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rubblebelt+Maaka)

In every single draft that we got to watch on the video coverage during the course of the weekend, we constantly saw [Rubblebelt Maaka](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rubblebelt+Maaka) going first- and second-pick, much to our surprise. We saw this once again when we watched Ari Lax drafting his Finals deck, taking the card second-pick over other good cards. Each time it happened, we became less and less surprised.

The Maaka is a great card, doubling as a red [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth) and a [Hill Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hill+Giant), it's incredibly versatile. It's also a monored card, a deceptively positive trait. As much as gets made of the desire to put yourself into a *Guildpact* guild, the fact that the Maaka allows you to decide either Gruul or Boros is an incredibly powerful thing. The bloodrush cost is low enough that you can continue on curve after blowing someone out in combat, something that hurts other bloodrush cards.

Players seemed to love these facts about the Maaka, and its draft stock rose tremendously this weekend. Though it was already acknowledged as a very good card, this weekend saw its popularity rise to a new level.

  
  

 

![Viashino Firstblade](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Viashino+Firstblade)

 

### 2\. [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade)

Grand Prix Providence finalists Ari Lax and Matt McCullough had a combined five copies of [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) in their final draft decks. It was enough to almost make us put the Firstblade in all five slots of this event's Top 5 cards!

  
 What makes [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) so good? It's really the impact that a three mana haste creature has, along with the burst of damage it provides. Boros loves haste to be able to add that "Surprise, I've got battalion" element to its strategy. [Skyknight Legionnaire](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skyknight+Legionnaire) has been doing an impeccable job of turning on the guild's mechanic a turn early, and [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) serves a similar purpose. Sometimes, the Firstblade even results in more damage, and it still sticks around after its first big attack to keep on swinging in.

  
  

 

![Blind Obedience](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Blind+Obedience)

 

### 1\. [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience)

What use is a [Viashino Firstblade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Firstblade) if it comes into play tapped? As Eric Phillipps proved against the five copies of the Boros common he faced in the Finals, not that great. This powerful enchantment hit play early against both of his aggressive Finals opponents, locking down their early rush and enabling him to assemble a defense, all the while also providing him a load of extort triggers.

The card was just as powerful in the aggressive decks seen around the room on Saturday, as multiple decks used Obedience to both keep blockers at bay for a turn, as well as providing the extra reach to finish things off.

Considering how devastating the aggressive draws Phillipps faced in the Finals would have been without the Obzedat's assistance, [Blind Obedience](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503135404/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blind+Obedience) has certainly bought earned its place in the Top 5 cards.
